Beurteilen Sie einen reinen Verkaufsbonus. Entwickeln Sie zwei Regeln für ethisch ausgerichtetes Wertemanagement in der Fernüberwachung und zwei Regeln für die Zusammenarbeit mit dem externen Servicepartner.

Musterlösung

Ein reiner Verkaufsbonus belohnt schnelle Kundengewinnung, aber nicht sichere Installation, Datenschutz oder nachhaltige Servicequalität. Beschwerden, Folgekosten und Reputationsschäden können steigen. Der Bonus sollte deshalb Kundenbindung, Erstlösungsquote, vollständige Freigaben und Beschwerdequote einbeziehen. Qualitäts- oder Compliance-Verstöße senken ihn unabhängig vom Absatz.

Für die Fernüberwachung gelten zweckgebundene Datenzugriffe nach dem Prinzip des geringsten erforderlichen Zugriffs. Neue Funktionen dürfen erst nach dokumentierter Datenschutz- und Sicherheitsfreigabe starten. Ein zweiter Verantwortlicher prüft die Freigabe.

Für den Partner gelten gleiche Sicherheits-, Qualitäts- und Vertraulichkeitspflichten. Zugriffe, Fehler und Änderungen werden nachvollziehbar protokolliert und regelmäßig geprüft. Vertrauliche Meldungen von Verstößen sind ohne Nachteil möglich.

Bewertungsraster

  • Anreizwirkung des reinen Verkaufsbons mit Qualitäts- und Compliancerisiken beurteilt 1 P
  • Mehrdimensionale Bonusregel mit Qualitäts- und Complianceanteil vorgeschlagen 1 P
  • Zwei Regeln für Datenschutz und Freigabe der Fernüberwachung genannt 1 P
  • Zwei Regeln für gleiche Standards und nachvollziehbare Partnerarbeit genannt 1 P

Häufige Prüflingsfehler

Istwerte wie Beschwerdezahl und Quote werden oft verwechselt. Beim Risikowert wird häufig nur der größte Einzelwert betrachtet. Die Fluktuation wird nicht mit dem durchschnittlichen Personalbestand berechnet, und die Nachfolgelücke wird unklar abgegrenzt.

Hinweise der Prüfer

Gute Antworten rechnen Kennzahlen und Abweichungen nachvollziehbar und verbinden sie mit Entscheidungen. Sie ordnen Risiken auch nach Patientensicherheit und gesetzlichen Pflichten. Wertemanagement wird durch Freigaben, Datenzugriff, Audits und widerspruchsfreie Anreize konkret.

Teilaufgabe 60 (Mock 10, Nr. 6 von 6): Integrated Decision for the Nordic Care Programme

Unternehmensspezifische Strategiefelder erkennen und ausgestalten · 10 Punkte English task

Aufgabe a (4 Punkte)

Calculate the financing gap at the start of the project and the cash balance at the end of the first year after that gap is financed. Calculate the debt service coverage ratio. Explain what the results mean for the board's approval decision.

Musterlösung

The investment requires 7.8 million euros. Internal funds of 2.6 million euros reduce the opening cash balance from 3.1 to 0.5 million euros. As the minimum balance is 2.4 million euros, the initial financing gap is 1.9 million euros.

The annual loan payment is approximately 1.0442 million euros. If the additional 1.9 million euros is provided as unremunerated equity at the start of the project, the cash balance is 2.4 million euros. At the end of the first year it is 0.5 plus 1.9 plus 1.85 minus 1.0442, which equals approximately 3.2058 million euros. The minimum balance is therefore maintained.

The debt service coverage ratio is 1.85 divided by 1.0442, or approximately 1.77. It exceeds the required level of 1.35. The unremunerated equity has no interest or repayment and therefore does not increase debt service. The cash flow covers the annual loan payment and liquidity is protected once the opening gap is financed.

The board should not approve the package unless at least 1.9 million euros of unremunerated equity is committed at the start of the project. A positive forecast does not remove an immediate payment obligation.

Bewertungsraster

  • Initial liquidity of 0.5 million euros and financing gap of 1.9 million euros calculated 1 P
  • End-of-year liquidity of approximately 3.2058 million euros after the opening unremunerated equity contribution is calculated 1 P
  • Debt service coverage ratio of approximately 1.77 calculated and compared with 1.35 1 P
  • Clear conclusion that at least 1.9 million euros of unremunerated equity without repayment is required at the start 1 P

Aufgabe b (3 Punkte)

Evaluate whether the planned launch of 1,050 units is compatible with capacity, the break-even result and the preferred central repair model. Use the capacity gap, contribution result and weighted location score. Name one compliance risk.

Musterlösung

The planned launch is not compatible with an immediate full rollout. Demand of 3,770 units exceeds practical capacity of 3,040 by 730 units. The Saturday shift can cover 600 units, leaving 130 units without capacity. With the first licence included in the bundle price, contribution per unit is 620 euros. The break-even quantity is 630,000 divided by 620, which gives 1,016.13 and therefore 1,017 units. At 1,050 units the contribution after fixed launch costs is 1,050 times 620 minus 630,000, or 21,000 euros.

At most 920 Nordic Care units can be produced with the Saturday shift: 3,040 plus 600 minus 2,720 domestic units. At 920 units the result is still 920 times 620 minus 630,000, or minus 59,600 euros. An initial 500 to 600 units would produce losses of 320,000 or 258,000 euros.

The central Lübeck model has the best weighted score of 3.75, although customer proximity is its main weakness. A local partner can provide field service while Klarsee keeps calibration, approvals and data control in Lübeck.

The main compliance risk is releasing the software before product, data protection and cybersecurity approvals are complete. A new release therefore requires documented approval and a fallback version.

Bewertungsraster

  • Capacity gap of 730 units and remaining gap of 130 units after the Saturday shift used correctly 1 P
  • Break-even of 1,017 units, 21,000 euros at 1,050 units and the negative 920-unit scenario integrated 1 P
  • Controlled rollout recommendation and one relevant compliance risk clearly stated 1 P

Aufgabe c (3 Punkte)

Recommend a phased decision to the management board and state four measurable approval conditions. Reconcile growth, liquidity, service quality and the needs of employees.

Musterlösung

I recommend that the board approve only a secured pilot. The annual practical total capacity, including the Saturday shift, must reach at least 3,770 units. Current demand consists of 1,050 Nordic Care units and 2,720 domestic units. Practical capacity is 3,040, and the Saturday shift adds 600, so 130 units remain without capacity. That remaining gap must be closed by additional owned capacity or a validated external manufacturing route. Without the full gap closure, the 920 Nordic Care units that can be supplied after domestic demand remain below the 1,017-unit break-even and produce a loss of about 59,600 euros. A qualified partner should handle local field service, while calibration, compliance and data control remain in Lübeck.

Four approval conditions should apply. First, practical total capacity must reach at least 3,770 units, with the final 130-unit gap closed before full launch, and the 1.9 million euros must be committed as unremunerated equity at the start so cash stays above 2.4 million euros through the first year. Second, product, data protection and cybersecurity approvals must be complete before customer installation. Third, the partner agreement must set measurable standards for response time, first-time resolution, complaints and confidential data access. Fourth, the company must train two service technicians, fill the training gap and name ready successors for all critical roles in the pilot.

The board should review progress after two quarters. The next stage may start only if practical total capacity is at least 3,770 units, liquidity remains above 2.4 million euros, complaints are at most 3 percent, first-time resolution is at least 90 percent and critical audits are current.

Bewertungsraster

  • Clear phased approval with practical total capacity raised from 3,640 to at least 3,770 units and the final 130-unit gap closed 1 P
  • Unremunerated equity of 1.9 million euros and the 2.4 million euro liquidity condition stated with correct timing 1 P
  • Four measurable conditions cover compliance, service quality and employees, with a review gate 1 P

Häufige Prüflingsfehler

Many candidates approve the project because the net present value is positive and ignore the cash shortfall. Others use full demand without the capacity limit or confuse break-even quantity with maximum capacity. Strong answers also link expansion to measurable quality and training conditions.

Hinweise der Prüfer

Strong answers bring financial, operational, organisational and compliance results together. They recommend a limited first stage, quantify the financing need, explain the capacity limit and make expansion dependent on measurable liquidity, quality, audit and qualification results.
